- Result: transcendental ideality **plus** empirical reality — never quote one alone.
- 2019 "transcendence" = a priori and transcendentally ideal status, not transcendent existence.
- Non-Euclidean geometry damages the transcendental exposition of space, not the metaphysical one.
- Asymmetry: only time is in every representation — the hinge of the Schematism.

#### CLOSING RECALL FLOW — THE TRANSCENDENTAL AESTHETIC

```text
SENSIBILITY IS RECEPTIVE -> it has PURE FORMS
   SPACE = form of OUTER sense | TIME = form of INNER sense -> and so of ALL
        |
        v
METAPHYSICAL EXPOSITION (what it is)
   1 not empirical + 2 necessary ....................... A PRIORI
   3 one space, parts by limitation + 4 given infinite .. INTUITION
        |
        v
TRANSCENDENTAL EXPOSITION (what it explains)
   SPACE -> GEOMETRY | TIME -> ARITHMETIC + pure doctrine of motion
        |
        +--> REJECTS NEWTON (real containers) and LEIBNIZ (confused relations)
        |    both treat space as ENCOUNTERED, not as the FORM of encounter
        |
        v
DOUBLE RESULT: TRANSCENDENTAL IDEALITY + EMPIRICAL REALITY
   -> transcendental idealism, NOT Berkeley (see Refutation, B274-9)
QUALIFICATION: non-Euclidean geometry damages the transcendental exposition
   of space; the metaphysical exposition stands
CARRY FORWARD: only TIME is in every representation -> the SCHEMATISM
ANSWER LINE: once space is the form of encounter, the Newton-Leibniz dispute
   dissolves rather than being decided
```

### SESSION 5 — THE METAPHYSICAL DEDUCTION AND THE TABLE OF THE TWELVE CATEGORIES

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition:** the understanding does only one thing — it judges — so if you can list every possible *form* a judgement can take, you have thereby listed every basic way the understanding can unify a sensory manifold; those basic ways are the categories.

**Technical definition:** the **metaphysical deduction** is the derivation of the pure concepts of the understanding (**categories**) from the logical functions of judgement. Twelve logical forms of judgement, in four groups of three, yield twelve categories in the same four groups — Quantity, Quality, Relation and Modality — each category being the concept of the synthesis of a manifold in accordance with one such logical function.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> The categories are not a shopping list of favourite metaphysical nouns; they are the *same* functions that organise the form of a judgement, now taken as rules for organising a sensory manifold — which is why Kant can claim completeness for the table, and why the completeness claim is also the table's most vulnerable point.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

- **the understanding is a faculty of judging** — the premise that generates the whole table
- **logical function of judgement → pure concept of the understanding** — the derivation, in six words
- **Quantity, Quality, Relation, Modality; three under each** — the architecture
- **Inherence and subsistence · causality and dependence · community (reciprocity)** — the Relation group, in Kant's own phrasing
- **metaphysical deduction ≠ transcendental deduction** — identification versus justification
- **the completeness claim** — where the strongest objection lands

**How to use them:** never present the table without its derivation. A table copied from memory earns very little; a table *derived* from the claim that the understanding is a faculty of judging earns the structure mark and sets up the objection you will then handle.

#### Plain-language visual — one faculty, one activity, twelve forms

```text
   THE UNDERSTANDING DOES EXACTLY ONE THING: IT JUDGES.
   "S is P", "if S then P", "all S are P", "some S are P", "S may be P"...
                              |
   Every judgement has a FORM, and the forms are FINITE and LISTABLE
   (this is the claim general logic had already made).
                              |
   Now apply the same forms not to CONCEPTS but to an intuited MANIFOLD.
   The form "if..., then..." applied to concepts is a hypothetical
   judgement. Applied to a manifold given in time, it becomes the rule:
   "this state follows that one ACCORDING TO A RULE" - i.e. CAUSALITY.
                              |
                              v
   SO: 12 LOGICAL FUNCTIONS OF JUDGEMENT  ->  12 CATEGORIES
   The categories are the SAME functions doing a DIFFERENT job.
```

**In simple words:** think of a machine that can fold paper in exactly twelve ways. Ask what shapes the machine can produce, and you have already answered by listing the folds. Kant's claim is that the understanding is such a machine: list the ways it can join a subject to a predicate, and you have listed the ways it can join a manifold into an object.

| Remember first | Meaning |
|---|---|
| One faculty, one activity | judging; everything else is derivative |
| Same functions, two employments | on concepts → logic; on a manifold → categories |
| Four groups of three | Quantity, Quality, Relation, Modality |
| The third in each group | is generated from the first two combined |
| Metaphysical ≠ transcendental | this table *identifies*; the next chapter *justifies* |

> **One-line memory:** the categories are the logical forms of judgement, redeployed as rules for making an object out of a manifold.

#### 🖼️ VISUAL — the table of judgements and the table of categories, side by side

```text
  LOGICAL FUNCTIONS OF JUDGEMENT        PURE CONCEPTS OF THE UNDERSTANDING
  (general logic, form only)            (the CATEGORIES, applied to a manifold)
 ======================================================================
  QUANTITY                              QUANTITY
    Universal   "All S are P"      ->     UNITY
    Particular  "Some S are P"     ->     PLURALITY
    Singular    "This S is P"      ->     TOTALITY
 ----------------------------------------------------------------------
  QUALITY                               QUALITY
    Affirmative "S is P"           ->     REALITY
    Negative    "S is not P"       ->     NEGATION
    Infinite    "S is non-P"       ->     LIMITATION
 ----------------------------------------------------------------------
  RELATION                              RELATION      <== THE EXAM GROUP
    Categorical "S is P"           ->     INHERENCE AND SUBSISTENCE
                                          (substantia et accidens)
    Hypothetical "if S then P"     ->     CAUSALITY AND DEPENDENCE
                                          (cause and effect)
    Disjunctive "S is either P     ->     COMMUNITY (RECIPROCITY between
                 or Q"                     agent and patient)
 ----------------------------------------------------------------------
  MODALITY                              MODALITY
    Problematic "S may be P"       ->     POSSIBILITY - IMPOSSIBILITY
    Assertoric  "S is P"           ->     EXISTENCE - NON-EXISTENCE
    Apodictic   "S must be P"      ->     NECESSITY - CONTINGENCY
 ======================================================================
  THREE STRUCTURAL FACTS WORTH ONE LINE EACH
  1. FOUR groups of THREE - and in each group the THIRD category arises
     from the combination of the first two (totality = plurality
     considered as unity; limitation = reality combined with negation;
     community = reciprocal causality; necessity = existence given
     through possibility itself).
  2. The RELATION group is the examinable one: SUBSTANCE answers Hume on
     substance, CAUSALITY answers Hume on causation, COMMUNITY grounds
     simultaneity. All three reappear as the THREE ANALOGIES.
  3. The categories are as yet UNSCHEMATISED - pure logical functions
     with no sensible sense. Until the Schematism supplies the schema,
     a category can be THOUGHT but determines no object.
 ======================================================================
  THE STANDING OBJECTION, AND WHERE TO PUT IT
  "Why exactly twelve? The derivation from the table of judgements is
  artificial." - pressed by Fichte, Hegel and later Kantians.
  KANT'S DEFENCE: the table of judgements is claimed to be complete and
  systematic, and no rival table has been offered that is more complete.
  HONEST VERDICT: the STRATEGY of the transcendental deduction does not
  depend on the INVENTORY. Detach the two - as Strawson does in his
  austere reading - and Kant's core result survives the objection.
```

⚠️ **Caption.** The last box is what converts recall into assessment. State the completeness objection, give Kant's defence, and then separate strategy from inventory; that three-move sequence is worth more than a perfectly reproduced table.

#### 1. The premise: the understanding is a faculty of judging

✅ Kant argues that all acts of the understanding can be reduced to judgements. A concept is never related to an object immediately; it is related to some other representation of the object, and the unity of that relation is a judgement. Therefore the functions of unity in judgement exhaust the functions of the understanding.

⚠️ This premise is doing enormous work, and it is worth stating explicitly. If the understanding did anything *other* than judge, the table could not be complete, and the metaphysical deduction would fail even if every entry in it were correct.

#### 2. From logical function to category

✅ In general logic the *form* of a judgement is considered in abstraction from all content. In transcendental logic the same form is considered as applied to a manifold **given in intuition**. The category is the concept of the synthesis of that manifold in accordance with the logical function.

Take the **hypothetical** form. In logic, "if p then q" merely relates two propositions. Applied to a manifold given in time, it becomes the rule that one state follows another *according to a rule* — and that is the category of **causality and dependence**. This single worked example is the most efficient way to show that the derivation is systematic rather than decorative.

#### 3. The table, group by group, with what each group does

| Group | Categories | What the group governs |
|---|---|---|
| **Quantity** | Unity, Plurality, Totality | the **extensive magnitude** of an appearance — that every appearance is an aggregate of homogeneous parts, which is why every appearance can be measured |
| **Quality** | Reality, Negation, Limitation | the **intensive magnitude** of a sensation — that every real in appearance has a degree, so that reality and its absence differ continuously and not by jumps |
| **Relation** | Inherence and subsistence (substance and accident); Causality and dependence (cause and effect); Community (reciprocity between agent and patient) | the **objective temporal relations** of appearances — duration, succession and simultaneity |
| **Modality** | Possibility–impossibility; Existence–non-existence; Necessity–contingency | not the object, but the **relation of the object to our faculty of cognition** — whether it agrees with the formal conditions of experience, is bound up with the material conditions, or is determined by them |

- ✅ The Modality group deserves a specific line. Kant is explicit that modality "contributes nothing to the content of the judgement" and instead concerns the value of the copula in relation to thought. That is why the modal categories will later yield the **Postulates of Empirical Thought** rather than a further law of nature.
- ⚠️ **Trap flagged early.** Because modality concerns the relation of the object to cognition, no modal category licenses an inference to a necessary *being*. This is exactly the confusion the Fourth Antinomy and the cosmological proof exploit — see Sessions 12 and 13.

#### 4. Why the third category in each group is not a fresh addition

✅ Kant notes that in every group the third category arises from the combination of the first two: **totality** is plurality considered as unity; **limitation** is reality combined with negation; **community** is the reciprocal causality of substances upon one another; **necessity** is the existence that is given through possibility itself.

⚠️ He immediately adds a caution: this does not make the third concept derivative in the sense of dispensable, because a special act of the understanding is required to produce it. Reporting both the pattern and the caution shows that the table has been understood rather than memorised.

#### 5. Metaphysical deduction versus transcendental deduction

| | Metaphysical deduction | Transcendental deduction |
|---|---|---|
| **Question answered** | *Which* concepts are the pure concepts of the understanding? | By what **right** do those concepts apply to objects given in sensibility? |
| **Method** | derivation from the complete table of logical functions of judgement | argument from the necessary unity of self-consciousness |
| **If it fails** | the inventory is wrong or incomplete | the entitlement is missing and the categories are, in Hume's phrase, bastards of the imagination |
| **Kant's own name for the problem** | *quid facti* — what the concepts are | *quid juris* — by what right |

⚠️ Confusing these two is listed by the owner file as a standing UPSC trap, and it is easy to avoid: **the metaphysical deduction identifies, the transcendental deduction justifies.**

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification

> **Claim.** The table of categories is an argued derivation rather than a list, and its argumentative character is what makes both its power and its vulnerability visible.
> **Named evidence.** Kant's premise is that all acts of the understanding are judgements, so the functions of unity in judgement exhaust the understanding; the twelve logical forms in four groups therefore yield twelve categories in the same four groups, the hypothetical form yielding causality and dependence, the categorical yielding inherence and subsistence, and the disjunctive yielding community.
> **Analysis.** Because the category is nothing but the logical function applied to a manifold given in intuition rather than to concepts, the derivation explains why these concepts and no others are pure — and it explains why the Relation group, not the others, generates the three Analogies that answer Hume about substance, causation and simultaneity.
> **Qualification.** The completeness of the table depends entirely on the completeness of the logic Kant inherited, and critics from Fichte and Hegel onward have called the derivation artificial; the honest position is that the strategy of the transcendental deduction does not depend on the inventory, so the argument that objectivity is a condition rather than a discovery survives even if the list of twelve does not.

#### Exam link

- **2018 Q2(b)** ("Understanding makes Nature") requires this table, because the "making" is precisely the imposition of these rules — but the *argument* comes in Session 6.
- **2024 Q1(b)** needs the Relation group to explain how causality can be a priori.
- **2022 Q1(e)** needs the metaphysical/transcendental distinction so that apperception is placed in the right chapter.

#### UPSC traps

| Trap | Correction |
|---|---|
| Reproducing the table with no derivation | The derivation *is* the answer; the table alone is a memory exercise |
| Confusing metaphysical with transcendental deduction | Identification versus justification |
| Calling the categories "innate ideas" | They are functions of synthesis, not contents; Locke's target is not Kant's doctrine |
| Treating modality as a property of objects | It concerns the relation of the object to our cognitive faculty |
| Defending the number twelve as self-evident | State the completeness objection and separate strategy from inventory |

#### Revision notes

- Premise: the understanding is a faculty of judging; hence the functions of judgement exhaust it.
- Category = the logical function of judgement applied to a manifold given in intuition.
- Four groups of three: Quantity (unity, plurality, totality); Quality (reality, negation, limitation); Relation (substance–accident, cause–effect, community); Modality (possibility, existence, necessity).
- The third in each group combines the first two; a special act of understanding is still required.
- Relation is the examinable group; it becomes the three Analogies.
- Unschematised categories are thinkable but determine no object.
- Objection: why twelve? Reply: claimed completeness of the table of judgements; honest verdict: strategy survives without the inventory.

#### CLOSING RECALL FLOW — THE METAPHYSICAL DEDUCTION

```text
UNDERSTANDING = A FACULTY OF JUDGING -> its functions are listable
        |
        v
12 LOGICAL FUNCTIONS OF JUDGEMENT, 4 GROUPS OF 3
   applied to CONCEPTS -> general logic
   applied to a MANIFOLD IN INTUITION -> TRANSCENDENTAL LOGIC
        |
        v
12 CATEGORIES
   QUANTITY unity/plurality/totality ....... extensive magnitude
   QUALITY  reality/negation/limitation .... intensive magnitude, degree
   RELATION substance-accident / cause-     objective TIME relations
            effect / community              -> THE THREE ANALOGIES
   MODALITY possible/actual/necessary ...... relation of object to COGNITION
        |
        +--> third of each group = first two combined (still a special act)
        +--> categories so far UNSCHEMATISED: thinkable, but no object
        |
        v
METAPHYSICAL DEDUCTION = which concepts (quid facti)
TRANSCENDENTAL DEDUCTION = by what right (quid juris)  -> NEXT SESSION
OBJECTION: why exactly twelve? | REPLY: claimed completeness | VERDICT:
   detach strategy from inventory and the core result stands
ANSWER LINE: the categories are the forms of judgement redeployed as rules
   for making an object out of a manifold
```

### SESSION 6 — THE TRANSCENDENTAL DEDUCTION, THE UNITY OF APPERCEPTION AND "UNDERSTANDING MAKES NATURE"

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition:** every experience I have is *mine*; they all belong to one consciousness that can, in principle, own each of them. Kant's argument is that they could not all be mine unless they were combined according to rules — and those rules are the categories, which is why the categories are guaranteed to apply to everything I can ever experience.

**Technical definition:** the **transcendental deduction of the categories** is the proof of their objective validity, conducted by showing that the **transcendental unity of apperception** — the possibility that the representation "I think" (*Ich denke*) accompany all my representations — is possible only if the manifold of intuition is synthesised according to the categories; the categories are therefore necessary conditions of any experience whatever, and hence necessarily valid of all objects of possible experience.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> The transcendental deduction is the strongest anti-sceptical argument in modern philosophy precisely because it asks the sceptic to concede almost nothing: only that his experiences are *his*. From that single admission Kant derives the rule-governed unity the sceptic thought he was entitled to doubt — which is why apperception is a *function*, never a soul, and why the Paralogisms follow immediately.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

- **the "I think" must be able to accompany all my representations (B131–2)** — note the modal *must be able to*
- **transcendental unity of apperception** — original, not derived; formal, not empirical
- **synthesis** — combination is never given by the senses; it is always an act
- **objective validity**, not psychological description
- **a function, not a thing** — the phrase that pre-empts the Paralogism
- **"Understanding makes Nature"** — with the *formal* restriction attached

**How to use them:** put the modal phrasing "*must be able to* accompany" in your answer verbatim. It is the difference between Kant's claim (a standing possibility of self-ascription) and the caricature (a permanent inner voice narrating one's life), and examiners notice.

#### Plain-language visual — the ownership argument

```text
   START WHERE EVEN THE SCEPTIC MUST START:
   "These experiences are MINE."
                    |
   What does that require?  Not that I constantly say "I think", but that
   I COULD attach "I think" to any of them.
                    |
   For that, they must be capable of being held together in ONE
   consciousness. A representation that could not be so held would be
   "nothing to me" - it would not be a representation OF MINE at all.
                    |
   But the senses NEVER give combination. Sensibility RECEIVES; it does
   not COMBINE. Combination (synthesis) is always an ACT of spontaneity.
                    |
   So the manifold must be combined - and combination that yields ONE
   consciousness must proceed ACCORDING TO RULES, not at random.
                    |
   Those rules are the CATEGORIES.
                    |
                    v
   THEREFORE: the categories apply necessarily to everything that can
   ever be an experience for me. Not because they copy reality, but
   because WITHOUT THEM THERE WOULD BE NO "ME" TO HAVE AN EXPERIENCE.
```

**In simple words:** imagine a library where books arrive continuously. If nothing is ever shelved by a rule, there is no library — only a heap. The claim is not that the librarian invents the books; the books arrive from outside. The claim is that without rule-governed shelving there is no *one* library at all, and the demand that everything be findable in one place is what forces the rules.

| Remember first | Meaning |
|---|---|
| The premise is minimal | only that my representations are mine |
| Combination is never given | synthesis is an act, always |
| Unity is the condition, not the result | the "I think" is presupposed, not discovered |
| It is formal, not empirical | not Hume's bundle, not Descartes' substance |
| The conclusion is restricted | valid of appearances, not of things in themselves |

> **One-line memory:** the categories are valid not because they mirror reality but because without them there could be no unified self-conscious experience at all.

#### 🖼️ VISUAL — the deduction as a chain, with the two flanking errors marked

```text
 ======================================================================
  THE PROBLEM (quid juris): a priori concepts ORIGINATE in the mind;
  objects are GIVEN by the senses. What entitles the first to legislate
  for the second? Hume's answer: nothing - so causal necessity is a
  fiction of the imagination.
 ======================================================================
  STEP 1  ALL MY REPRESENTATIONS MUST BE ABLE TO BE MINE
          "The I think must be able to accompany all my representations"
          (B131-2). Otherwise something would be represented in me that
          could not be thought - i.e. it would be nothing to me.
            |
  STEP 2  THIS UNITY IS *ORIGINAL* AND *TRANSCENDENTAL*
          Original: not derived from a prior unity.
          Transcendental: a CONDITION of experience, not an item IN it.
            |
  STEP 3  COMBINATION IS NEVER GIVEN BY THE SENSES
          "Combination does not lie in the objects and cannot be
          borrowed from them by perception." Synthesis is an act of
          SPONTANEITY - the understanding, aided by the productive
          imagination.
            |
  STEP 4  SYNTHESIS THAT PRODUCES ONE CONSCIOUSNESS MUST FOLLOW RULES
          A random assembly yields no unity; unity requires that the
          manifold be combined in a determinate, repeatable way.
            |
  STEP 5  THOSE RULES ARE THE CATEGORIES
          The category just IS the concept of the synthesis of a
          manifold according to a logical function of judgement
          (Session 5).
            |
  STEP 6  THEREFORE the categories are OBJECTIVELY VALID of every
          object of possible experience - because an object simply IS
          "that in the concept of which the manifold of a given
          intuition is UNITED".
 ======================================================================
  TWO FLANKING ERRORS THE ARGUMENT RULES OUT AT ONCE
  <-- HUME'S ERROR: he looked for the self among IMPRESSIONS and,
      finding none, dissolved it into a bundle. But the "I think" is
      not an impression; it is the FORM of all thinking and therefore
      cannot appear IN experience, being the condition OF it.
  --> RATIONAL PSYCHOLOGY'S ERROR: from the unity of the "I think" it
      infers a simple, permanent, immaterial SOUL-SUBSTANCE. But the
      "I think" is a FUNCTION, not a thing; the inference equivocates
      between a logical subject and a real substance. This is the
      PARALOGISM (Session 11).
 ======================================================================
  WHAT APPERCEPTION IS AND IS NOT
   IS:      formal | original | transcendental | a condition | a function
            the unity through which a manifold becomes ONE experience
   IS NOT:  an inner observation | an impression | Hume's bundle |
            a substance | a knowledge of myself as I am in myself
   NOTE:    Kant grants that I know myself only as I APPEAR to myself
            through inner sense, not as I am - the self is no exception
            to transcendental idealism.
 ======================================================================
```

⚠️ **Caption.** The two flanking errors are the highest-value content in this subtopic: they let one argument simultaneously answer Hume (Topic 03) and set up the Paralogisms (Session 11).

#### 1. The problem the deduction must solve

✅ Kant borrows a distinction from jurists. A *quid facti* question asks what is actually the case; a *quid juris* question asks by what right. The metaphysical deduction settled the first: these twelve concepts are the pure concepts of the understanding. The transcendental deduction must settle the second: how can concepts that originate wholly in the mind be valid of objects that are given to us from elsewhere?

✅ Empirical concepts pose no such problem, because they are drawn from the very experience they are then used to describe. The pure concepts are exactly those that are **not** so drawn — which is why, as Kant notes, Hume was entirely right that experience cannot supply them and entirely wrong about the consequence.

#### 2. The argument, step by step

1. **The premise.** "The *I think* must be able to accompany all my representations; for otherwise something would be represented in me that could not be thought at all, which is as much as to say that the representation would either be impossible or else at least would be nothing for me" (B131–2). ✅ The modal formulation matters: Kant does not claim that I actually think "I think" alongside each perception, only that I must be *able* to.
2. **The unity is original.** This apperception is not derived from any prior unity; it is the highest point to which all use of the understanding must be attached. Kant calls it *original* apperception and *pure* apperception, to distinguish it from the empirical consciousness that accompanies particular states.
3. **Combination is an act.** "The combination (*conjunctio*) of a manifold in general can never come to us through the senses." Sensibility is receptive; it delivers a manifold but never the unity of that manifold. Every combination is an act of spontaneity, exercised by the understanding through the productive imagination.
4. **Unity requires rule.** For a manifold to be combined in *one* consciousness, the combination cannot be arbitrary. The representations must be united in a way that is determinate and repeatable — that is, in accordance with a rule.
5. **The rules are the categories.** The category is precisely the concept of a synthesis of the manifold in accordance with a logical function of judgement. Therefore the rules under which any manifold must be combined if it is to belong to one self-consciousness are the categories.
6. **Objective validity follows.** An object, Kant writes, is "that in the concept of which the manifold of a given intuition is *united*." So to be an object at all, for us, is to be a manifold unified under categories. Their validity for all objects of possible experience is therefore not an empirical discovery but a condition.

#### 3. Apperception and space and time — the exact relation, for the 2022 answer

- ✅ **Sensibility** supplies the pure forms — space and time — **in** which a manifold can be given at all.
- ✅ **Apperception** supplies the requirement that this manifold be **combinable in one self-consciousness**.
- ✅ **Categories** are the rules of synthesis through which a manifold given in space and time can be represented as an object for one "I think".
- ⚠️ **The precise caution the 2022 question invites.** The transcendental exposition of space and time does **not** by itself deduce apperception. The architecture runs: the Aesthetic supplies the forms of intuition; the Deduction justifies the categories through apperceptive unity; the Schematism mediates their temporal application. Apperception unifies what sensibility gives — it neither creates the sensible matter nor proves a soul-substance. A script that says "the transcendental exposition proves apperception" has inverted the architecture.
- ✅ **Why the question links them anyway.** Because the unity is empty without something to unify. Space and time deliver a manifold that is already ordered but not yet *objectively* determined; apperception is the demand that this manifold be one experience; the categories are the rules that meet the demand. That is the connection the examiner is testing.

#### 4. "Understanding makes Nature" — the 2018 dictum, with its restriction

✅ **The statement.** The understanding "makes" nature in the sense that nature, **formally considered** as the law-governed unity of appearances, is constituted by the categories and principles under which alone a manifold can count as objective experience.

✅ **The argument in three lines.** Sensations alone are unordered. Synthesis under categories such as substance, causality and community yields objects standing in objective temporal relations, and hence universal laws. Therefore experienced nature necessarily conforms to the conditions of possible experience.

❌ **What it does not mean.** It does not mean that the understanding creates the **matter** of sensation; it does not mean that it produces **particular empirical laws** — those must be discovered; and it does not mean that things in themselves are made by us. It is emphatically not private imagination: the constitution is transcendental and therefore public.

⚠️ **The distinction that earns the mark.** Kant secures the **universal form** of nature a priori while leaving **particular laws** to empirical investigation. The dictum therefore expresses transcendental constitution *plus* empirical realism, not idealist creation. ❓ **Objection and reply.** The objection is that this collapses nature into mind; Kant's reply is that transcendental idealism *is* empirical realism — appearances are publicly real, even though they are known only under our forms of cognition.

#### 5. Against Hume and against rational psychology — one argument, two targets

| Target | Their claim | Kant's reply |
|---|---|---|
| **Hume** | introspection finds no impression of a self, so the self is a bundle of perceptions | he searched for the self *within* experience; the "I think" is the form of all thinking and cannot appear in experience because it is the condition of experience ⚠️ |
| **Rational psychology** | from the "I think" we may infer a substantial, simple, identical, immaterial soul | the "I think" is a **function**, not a thing; the inference confuses the logical unity of apperception with a real, knowable object — a *paralogism* ✅ |
| **Both together** | ⚠️ Kant's position is a genuine third option: the self as **condition** rather than as content or as substance. It is neither found nor proved; it is presupposed. |

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification

> **Claim.** The transcendental deduction establishes the objective validity of the categories from a premise the sceptic cannot refuse, namely that his representations are his own.
> **Named evidence.** Kant's premise is that "the *I think* must be able to accompany all my representations" (B131–2); he then argues that combination can never come to us through the senses but is always an act of spontaneity, and that a manifold can belong to one consciousness only if it is combined according to rules, those rules being the categories, since an object is "that in the concept of which the manifold of a given intuition is united".
> **Analysis.** Because the argument runs from the unity of self-consciousness rather than from the nature of objects, it delivers necessity without appealing to any experienced connection — which is why it answers Hume without contradicting him about impressions, and why the same result forbids the rational psychologist's inference from a formal unity to a soul-substance.
> **Qualification.** The conclusion is exactly as wide as its premise: the categories are valid of objects **as they can be given to us**, so the deduction secures objectivity within possible experience while leaving things in themselves untouched, and it does not by itself show *how* a pure category reaches a temporal appearance — that gap is the work of the Schematism.

#### Exam link

- **2022 Q1(e), 10 marks** is answered by §2 and §3, with the architectural caution in §3 as the closing line.
- **2018 Q2(b), 15 marks** takes its first limb entirely from §4, including the ❌ denials and the form/particular-law distinction.
- **2024 Q1(b)** uses §5 to state precisely what Kant concedes to Hume and what he denies.
- **2020 Q2(a)** — routed to Topic 03 but naming Kant — is answered from §5's first row: report the empiricist argument first, then Kant's reply.

#### UPSC traps

| Trap | Correction |
|---|---|
| "Kant proves the existence of the self" | He proves a formal condition; the substantial self is precisely what the Paralogisms forbid |
| Dropping the modal "must be able to" | Kant's claim is a standing possibility of self-ascription, not a continuous inner commentary |
| Treating apperception as introspection | It is not observed at all; it is the condition of anything being observed as mine |
| Reading "Understanding makes Nature" as creation | Form, not matter; universal law, not particular laws |
| Saying the transcendental exposition deduces apperception | The Aesthetic gives forms; the Deduction justifies categories through apperception |
| Presenting the deduction as psychology | It is an argument about conditions, not a description of mental processing |

#### Revision notes

- *Quid facti* (which concepts) versus *quid juris* (by what right).
- Premise: "the *I think* must be able to accompany all my representations" (B131–2).
- Combination is never given by the senses; synthesis is spontaneity.
- Unity of consciousness requires rule-governed synthesis; the rules are the categories.
- Object = that in the concept of which a given manifold is united.
- Apperception is formal, original, transcendental — a function, not a substance, not Hume's bundle.
- Architecture: Aesthetic gives forms → Deduction justifies categories through apperception → Schematism mediates application.
- "Understanding makes Nature" = nature *formally* considered; not matter, not particular laws, not private imagination.
- I know myself only as I appear to myself through inner sense.

#### CLOSING RECALL FLOW — THE TRANSCENDENTAL DEDUCTION

```text
QUID JURIS: by what right do mind-originating concepts apply to given objects?
        |
        v
PREMISE (B131-2): "the I THINK must be ABLE TO accompany all my representations"
        |
        +--> combination is NEVER given by the senses -> SYNTHESIS is an ACT
        +--> one consciousness requires RULE-GOVERNED synthesis
        +--> those rules ARE the CATEGORIES
        |
        v
OBJECT = that in the concept of which a given manifold is UNITED
   -> categories are OBJECTIVELY VALID of every object of possible experience
        |
        +--> AGAINST HUME: he sought the self IN experience; the "I think" is
        |    the FORM of thinking, condition not content
        +--> AGAINST RATIONAL PSYCHOLOGY: a FUNCTION, not a substance ->
        |    PARALOGISM
        |
        v
"UNDERSTANDING MAKES NATURE" = nature FORMALLY considered, as law-governed
   unity of appearances | NOT the matter of sensation | NOT particular
   empirical laws | NOT private imagination
ARCHITECTURE: Aesthetic (forms) -> Deduction (categories via apperception) ->
   Schematism (temporal application)
ANSWER LINE: the categories are valid not because they mirror reality but
   because without them there would be no unified self-conscious experience
```

### SESSION 7 — THE SCHEMATISM: HOW A PURE CATEGORY REACHES A TEMPORAL APPEARANCE

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition:** the categories are purely intellectual and appearances are purely sensory, so they have nothing in common; Kant's solution is a go-between that is intellectual on one side and sensory on the other, and the only thing that fits is a **rule about time**.

**Technical definition:** the **transcendental schema** is a mediating representation, produced by the productive imagination (*produktive Einbildungskraft*), that is homogeneous with the category in one respect and with appearance in another; every such schema is a **transcendental determination of time** (*transzendentale Zeitbestimmung*), and a category possesses objective application only in and through its schema. An unschematised category is a mere logical function with no sense for objects.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> The Schematism is not an appendix but the load-bearing joint of the *Critique*: it converts twelve logical functions into rules of time-determination and thereby converts the Deduction's abstract entitlement into the concrete Principles — at the cost of Kant's own admission that the system's most important operation is the one he cannot display.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

- **heterogeneity** — the problem, named in one word
- **the third thing (*ein Drittes*)** — the form of the solution
- **transcendental determination of time** — the content of the solution
- **schema is a rule/procedure, image is a product** — the discrimination that decides the section
- **productive imagination** — the faculty that produces schemata
- **"an art concealed in the depths of the human soul" (A141/B180–1)** — Kant's own concession
- **time-series, time-content, time-order, time-sum-total** — the four aspects

**How to use them:** state the problem before the solution. A candidate who begins "the schema is a transcendental determination of time" has given a definition; a candidate who begins "categories and appearances are utterly unlike, so subsumption is impossible without a mediator" has posed a problem the examiner can watch being solved.

#### Plain-language visual — why an ordinary concept needs no bridge, and a category does

```text
   ORDINARY CASE - NO PROBLEM
   concept PLATE  <----- shares ROUNDNESS ----->  this round dish
   The concept and the object are HOMOGENEOUS: something in the concept
   is also literally in the intuition. Subsumption is straightforward.

   KANTIAN CASE - THE PROBLEM
   category CAUSE  <---- shares NOTHING ---->  this sequence of colours
   The category is PURE, intellectual, non-sensible. The appearance is
   sensible, in space and time. Nothing in "cause" is literally IN any
   appearance. So how can any appearance ever fall UNDER it?

   THE FORM OF THE ANSWER
   category  <--- intellectual side --- SCHEMA --- sensible side ---> appearance
                                  (a RULE about TIME)
   The schema is not half-concept and half-picture. It is a priori and
   rule-governed (hence like the category) AND present in every
   appearance whatever (hence like intuition).
```

**In simple words:** a legal statute cannot arrest anybody. A police procedure can. The statute supplies the authority, the procedure supplies the method, and the procedure is written in the only medium in which every event in the country occurs — the clock. Kant's schema is the procedure; time is the medium; and this is why the Deduction alone was not enough.

| Remember first | Meaning |
|---|---|
| The problem is heterogeneity | not vagueness, not difficulty — utter unlikeness |
| A mediator must share with both | a priori like the category, universal in intuition like time |
| Space fails, time succeeds | my inner states are not in space; everything is in time |
| Schema = rule, image = product | this one line saves the section |
| Kant concedes opacity | "an art concealed in the depths of the human soul" |

> **One-line memory:** without a schema a category can be thought but determines no object — so the Schematism is where the *Critique* either earns its keep or fails.

#### 🖼️ VISUAL — the numbered argument, the four time-aspects, and the schema/image discrimination

```text
 ======================================================================
  WHY TIME AND NOTHING ELSE - THE ARGUMENT IN SIX STEPS
 ======================================================================
  1  Subsumption requires HOMOGENEITY between the subsuming concept and
     the subsumed representation.
  2  Categories and appearances are HETEROGENEOUS. So a MEDIATOR is
     required.
  3  The mediator must be (a) UNIVERSAL AND A PRIORI, like the category,
     and (b) CONTAINED IN EVERY APPEARANCE, like intuition.
  4  TIME uniquely satisfies both. It is a pure a priori form (hence
     rule-governed and universal) AND it is the form of INNER sense, so
     every representation whatever - outer as well as inner - is in time.
     SPACE FAILS (b): outer objects are in space, but my inner states
     are not.
  5  Therefore the mediating schemata are DETERMINATIONS OF TIME,
     produced by the PRODUCTIVE IMAGINATION.
  6  Therefore a category has objective application ONLY through its
     schema; an UNSCHEMATISED category is a mere logical function with
     no sense for objects.
 ======================================================================
  SCHEMA IS NOT AN IMAGE - KANT'S OWN THREE EXAMPLES
 ----------------------------------------------------------------------
   CASE      IMAGE (a product)              SCHEMA (a rule/procedure)
   TRIANGLE  any drawn triangle is          the RULE of synthesis by
             DETERMINATELY right-angled     which imagination can
             or oblique, equilateral or     construct a triangle IN
             scalene                        GENERAL - never adequate to
                                            any single image
   FIVE      • • • • •                      "a METHOD of representing a
                                            multitude in one image" -
                                            successive homogeneous
                                            addition
   DOG       this picture of this dog       the rule by which imagination
                                            "can delineate the figure of
                                            a four-footed animal in
                                            general"
   >>> THE MOST EXAMINABLE TRAP IN THE WHOLE SECTION: writing "the schema
       is a mental picture that resembles both" loses the section.
 ======================================================================
  THE FOUR SCHEMATA - FOUR ASPECTS OF TIME (A142-5 / B182-4)
 ----------------------------------------------------------------------
   GROUP      ASPECT OF TIME        SCHEMA
   QUANTITY   time-SERIES           NUMBER - successive addition of
              (Zeitreihe)           homogeneous units; the generation of
                                    time itself
   QUALITY    time-CONTENT          DEGREE / intensive magnitude - the
              (Zeitinhalt)          filling of time; continuous
                                    transition from sensation to its
                                    negation
   RELATION   time-ORDER            SUBSTANCE = permanence of the real in
              (Zeitordnung)         time | CAUSE = succession of the
                                    manifold according to a rule |
                                    COMMUNITY = coexistence of
                                    determinations according to a
                                    universal rule
   MODALITY   time-SUM-TOTAL        POSSIBLE = agreement with the
              (Zeitinbegriff)       conditions of time at SOME time |
                                    ACTUAL = existence at a DETERMINATE
                                    time | NECESSARY = existence at ALL
                                    times
   MNEMONIC "SCOR-S": Series - Content - Order - Sum-total
 ======================================================================
  WHERE THE SCHEMATA GO NEXT: the three Relation schemata ARE the three
  ANALOGIES OF EXPERIENCE in operation (Session 8). That is the best
  defence against the charge that the chapter is redundant.
 ======================================================================
```

⚠️ **Caption.** Notice the elegance the examiner is looking for: four category-groups, four aspects of time, and the Relation row turning directly into the three Analogies. The architecture is the argument.

#### 1. The problem Kant sets himself

✅ In every subsumption of an object under a concept, the representation of the object must be **homogeneous** with the concept — the concept must contain something that is represented in the object. The empirical concept *plate* and the intuited round dish share roundness, so the dish falls under the concept without difficulty.

✅ But **no category is like any appearance**. Causality, substance, plurality are pure, intellectual and non-sensible; appearances are sensible and given in space and time. The Transcendental Deduction proved that the categories **must** apply. It did not explain **how** they can apply at all.

⚠️ The two chapters therefore answer different questions, and saying so pre-empts the standard objection that the Schematism is redundant.

#### 2. Kant's solution — the third thing

✅ There must be a **third thing** (*ein Drittes*) homogeneous with the category on one side and with appearance on the other: **in one respect intellectual, in another sensible**. That mediator is the **transcendental schema**, and every schema is a **transcendental determination of time**.

⚠️ Note the precise wording. The schema is not a mixture, half-intellectual and half-sensible. Time is homogeneous with the category in one respect — it is a priori, universal and rule-governed — and homogeneous with appearance in another — it is the form under which every appearance is given. The homogeneity is in **different respects**, not in parts.

#### 3. The four presuppositions, stated so they can be examined

| | Presupposition | Why it matters |
|---|---|---|
| **P1** | Time is the form of inner sense and therefore of **all** representations without exception | if any representation could be non-temporal, the bridge does not span ⚠️ |
| **P2** | Subsumption strictly requires homogeneity | ⚠️ an objector may reply that empirical subsumption does not clearly require it either, in which case the problem may be over-stated |
| **P3** | The productive imagination is a genuine third faculty, not reducible to sensibility or understanding | the schema needs a producer that belongs to neither side alone |
| **P4** | Categories have no *sense* apart from schemata | this is exactly why the unschematised category can be **thought** — the negative noumenon — but yields no knowledge ✅ |

✅ P4 is the doctrinally richest of the four: it is the reason Kant can say that we can *think* things in themselves although we cannot *know* them. Thinking requires only the logical function; knowing requires the schema and hence a possible intuition.

#### 4. The strongest objections and Kant's replies

| Objection | Reply | Residual |
|---|---|---|
| The schema, being both intellectual and sensible, is as puzzling as what it explains — the heterogeneity problem simply reappears inside the schema | Time is not a *mixture* of the two but the one form that is a priori *and* universally instantiated in intuition; it is homogeneous with each in a different respect, not partly each | ❓ Kant's own "concealed art" admission concedes that the mechanism is not exhibited |
| **Hegel:** the whole apparatus is an external machinery of faculties artificially divided and then artificially rejoined; a genuine philosophy would exhibit thought and intuition as moments of one self-developing concept | Kant: the division is not stipulated but forced by the facts — sensibility is receptive, understanding is spontaneous, and no act of will can make the one do the other's work | ⚠️ This is the deepest objection available and should be flagged in any 20-mark answer |
| The chapter is redundant given the Deduction (the Warren and Paton "patchwork" debate) | The Deduction proves *that* the categories apply; only the Schematism supplies the *rule of application* which the Principles then deploy — without schemata there could be no Analogies | ⚠️ The best defence is functional: the Analogies literally **are** the schemata of Relation in operation |

✅ Kant himself calls schematism "an art concealed in the depths of the human soul, whose true operations we can divine from nature and lay unveiled before our eyes only with difficulty" (A141/B180–1). ⚠️ Renderings vary; attribute with the A/B reference and the phrase is safe. Quoting it is not a weakness in your answer — it is Kant's candour, and reporting it demonstrates that you have read the section rather than a summary of it.

#### 5. Executable verdict for a 15- or 20-mark answer

⚠️ "The Schematism is not an appendix but the load-bearing joint of the *Critique*: it converts twelve logical functions into rules of time-determination, and thereby converts the Deduction's abstract entitlement into the concrete Principles. Its cost is Kant's own admission that the productive imagination's procedure is 'an art concealed in the depths of the human soul' — the system's most important operation is the one Kant cannot display."

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification

> **Claim.** The Schematism solves a problem the Transcendental Deduction had left standing, and it solves it by making time the sole medium of categorial application.
> **Named evidence.** Kant argues that subsumption requires homogeneity, that categories and appearances are heterogeneous, that the required mediator must be both a priori and present in every appearance, and that time alone satisfies both conditions because it is the form of inner sense and therefore of all representations; the schemata are accordingly number, degree, permanence, succession according to a rule, coexistence according to a rule, and existence at some, at a determinate, or at all times.
> **Analysis.** Because the schema is a rule of time-determination and not an image, the account explains how a purely logical function can constrain a sensory manifold without ever resembling it, and it explains why space could not do the work — outer objects are in space, but inner states are not, so space lacks the universality the mediator requires.
> **Qualification.** Kant's own description of the procedure as "an art concealed in the depths of the human soul" (A141/B180–1) concedes that the mechanism is postulated rather than exhibited, and Hegel's objection that the faculties were artificially separated before being artificially rejoined remains unanswered on Kant's own terms; the chapter therefore succeeds internally while remaining vulnerable externally.

#### Exam link

- No PYQ from 2018 to 2025 asks about the Schematism directly, but it is **required supporting doctrine** for two limbs that are asked: how causality can be a priori (**2024 Q1(b)**) and how the understanding constitutes nature (**2018 Q2(b)**).
- It is also the cleanest way to explain, in one sentence, why the categories are valid only of appearances — which every Antinomy and God-proof answer needs.
- ⚠️ Do not devote a whole answer to schematism unless the question asks for it. Two or three sentences, placed correctly, are worth more than a digression.

#### UPSC traps

| Trap | Correction |
|---|---|
| "The schema is a mental image resembling both" | The schema is a **rule/procedure**; the image is a **product** — this is the section's decisive discrimination |
| Explaining the schema without stating the heterogeneity problem | The solution is unintelligible without the problem |
| Saying space also supplies schemata | Space is the form of outer sense only; inner states are not in space |
| Treating the Schematism as merely decorative | Without schemata there are no Analogies and hence no Principles |
| Hiding Kant's "concealed art" admission | Reporting it is evidence of primary reading and pre-empts the examiner's objection |

#### Revision notes

- Problem: categories and appearances are heterogeneous, so subsumption is impossible without a mediator.
- Solution: the transcendental schema, a **transcendental determination of time**, produced by the productive imagination.
- Why time: it is a priori *and* the form of inner sense, so present in every representation; space fails this test.
- Schema ≠ image: triangle, five, dog — rule versus product.
- Four aspects: time-series (number), time-content (degree), time-order (permanence, succession by rule, coexistence by rule), time-sum-total (at some/at a determinate/at all times). Mnemonic SCOR-S.
- P4: no schema, no sense for objects — hence the unschematised category is thinkable but not knowable.
- Objections: heterogeneity reappears; Hegel on artificial division; redundancy — best defence is functional.
- Kant's concession: "an art concealed in the depths of the human soul" (A141/B180–1).

#### CLOSING RECALL FLOW — THE SCHEMATISM

```text
PROBLEM: categories are PURE and intellectual; appearances are SENSIBLE
   -> HETEROGENEOUS -> subsumption impossible without a mediator
        |
        v
MEDIATOR MUST BE: (a) a priori and universal like the CATEGORY
                  (b) present in EVERY appearance like INTUITION
        |
        v
ONLY TIME QUALIFIES (form of INNER sense -> in every representation)
   SPACE FAILS (b): inner states are not in space
        |
        v
SCHEMA = TRANSCENDENTAL DETERMINATION OF TIME, made by the PRODUCTIVE
   IMAGINATION | SCHEMA = RULE, IMAGE = PRODUCT (triangle, five, dog)
        |
        v
FOUR ASPECTS "SCOR-S": SERIES -> number | CONTENT -> degree |
   ORDER -> permanence, succession by rule, coexistence by rule |
   SUM-TOTAL -> at some time / at a determinate time / at all times
        |
        +--> RELATION schemata BECOME the three ANALOGIES (next session)
        +--> P4: no schema, no sense for objects -> unschematised category
             is THINKABLE (negative noumenon) but not KNOWABLE
        |
        v
OBJECTIONS: heterogeneity reappears | HEGEL: artificial division then
   artificial rejoining | redundancy (Warren, Paton) -> functional defence
CONCESSION: "an art concealed in the depths of the human soul" A141/B180-1
ANSWER LINE: the Schematism is the load-bearing joint that turns entitlement
   into application - and the one operation Kant cannot display
```

### SESSION 8 — THE ANALOGIES OF EXPERIENCE: SUBSTANCE, CAUSALITY AND COMMUNITY, THE REAL ANSWER TO HUME

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition:** we never see time itself — only things. So whenever we claim that something *really* lasted, *really* happened next, or *really* happened at the same moment, we must be relying on a rule found among the things. The three Analogies state the three rules.

**Technical definition:** the **Analogies of Experience** are the synthetic a priori principles corresponding to the schematised categories of **Relation**. Their general principle is that "experience is possible only through the representation of a necessary connection of perceptions" (B218). They are *regulative* with respect to intuition — they do not tell us what we shall perceive — but *constitutive* with respect to experience: whatever is perceived must be ordered as substance, cause and community if it is to count as an **objective** temporal order at all.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> The Analogies are Kant's true reply to empiricism, and their engine is a single premise Hume never disputed: **time itself cannot be perceived**. If objective duration, succession and simultaneity cannot be read off time, they must be established by rules found in appearances — and those rules are substance, causality and reciprocity.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

- **"time itself cannot be perceived" (A176/B219, A183/B226)** — the master premise
- **proxy for time** — one Analogy per mode of time
- **alteration versus coming-to-be** — the First Analogy's decisive distinction
- **the house and the ship** — the canonical example
- **reversible versus irreversible order of apprehension**
- **entitlement, not particular laws** — the exact size of Kant's victory over Hume
- **Strawson's "non-sequitur of numbing grossness"** — the strongest objection, named

**How to use them:** lead with the master premise. Every candidate can list three Analogies; very few explain why there are exactly three and why they are needed at all. The answer to both is that time has exactly three modes and none of them is perceivable.

#### Plain-language visual — three modes of time, three proxies

```text
   TIME HAS EXACTLY THREE MODES:
        DURATION (how long)   SUCCESSION (what after what)
                        SIMULTANEITY (what alongside what)
                              |
   BUT TIME ITSELF IS NEVER PERCEIVED. You never see "an hour". You see
   a clock - which is a THING.
                              |
   So each mode needs a PROXY found among appearances:
        DURATION       ->  something that PERSISTS ......... SUBSTANCE
        SUCCESSION     ->  a RULE fixing which comes first .. CAUSALITY
        SIMULTANEITY   ->  MUTUAL determination ............. COMMUNITY
                              |
   THREE MODES, THREE PROXIES, THREE ANALOGIES. That is why there are
   exactly three, and why they are Relation categories and not others.
```

**In simple words:** you cannot photograph "next week". You can only photograph things and infer the time from how they behave. If nothing whatever persisted, you could not say that anything had *changed* rather than been replaced; if nothing followed by rule, you could not say the ship really moved rather than that you happened to look downstream second. Time-talk is parasitic on thing-talk, and the three Analogies are the three rules of parasitism.

| Remember first | Meaning |
|---|---|
| One premise runs all three | time itself cannot be perceived |
| Three modes of time | duration, succession, simultaneity |
| Regulative for intuition | they predict no particular perception |
| Constitutive for experience | nothing counts as objective without them |
| The victory is about entitlement | not about knowing particular causal laws |

> **One-line memory:** objectivity is rule-governed time-determination — and anyone who accepts the distinction between an objective event and a subjective sequence has already conceded more than Hume allowed.

#### 🖼️ VISUAL — the three Analogies, the ship and the house, and the objection

```text
 ======================================================================
  GENERAL PRINCIPLE (B218): "Experience is possible only through the
  representation of a NECESSARY CONNECTION of perceptions."
  MASTER PREMISE (A176/B219, A183/B226): TIME ITSELF CANNOT BE PERCEIVED.
 ======================================================================
   ANALOGY   MODE OF TIME   PRINCIPLE                     PROXY
   FIRST     DURATION /     In all change of appearances  the persisting
             PERSISTENCE    SUBSTANCE PERSISTS, and its   substratum
                            quantum in nature is neither
                            increased nor diminished (B224)
   SECOND    SUCCESSION     All alterations occur in      the causal rule
                            accordance with the law of
                            the connection of CAUSE and
                            EFFECT (B232)
   THIRD     SIMULTANEITY   All substances, insofar as    mutual causal
                            they can be perceived as      community
                            simultaneous in space, are    (commercium)
                            in thoroughgoing RECIPROCITY
                            (B256)
 ======================================================================
  THE CANONICAL EXAMPLE (A190-3 / B235-8)
 ----------------------------------------------------------------------
   VIEWING A HOUSE            roof -> door -> basement, OR
                              basement -> door -> roof
                              I CHOOSE the order .... REVERSIBLE
                              => a merely SUBJECTIVE succession of my
                                 perceptions of a COEXISTING object
   WATCHING A SHIP DRIFT      upstream position -> downstream position
                              I CANNOT choose ....... IRREVERSIBLE
                              => an OBJECTIVE succession - an EVENT
 ----------------------------------------------------------------------
   THE INFERENCE: apprehension is ALWAYS successive in BOTH cases, so
   succession-of-perceptions cannot by itself constitute perception of
   an objective succession. The difference must lie in the NECESSITY of
   the order - and necessity of order can come only from a RULE.
   That rule is the concept of CAUSE: "everything that happens
   presupposes something upon which it follows ACCORDING TO A RULE"
   (A189).
 ======================================================================
  THE STRONGEST OBJECTION - NAME IT, THEN ADJUDICATE
   STRAWSON (The Bounds of Sense, 1966): a "NON-SEQUITUR OF NUMBING
   GROSSNESS". Kant slides from
     (i) the order of my perceptions is not up to me - it is determined
         by the object
   to
     (ii) the event is NECESSITATED BY A CAUSAL LAW.
   Conceptual necessity in the order of APPREHENSION does not yield
   causal necessitation IN NATURE.
   GUYER presses a related point: at most Kant shows we need SOME rule,
   not the UNIVERSAL causal law.
   REPLY (Allison, transcendental-idealist reading): Kant is arguing
   about the conditions under which a succession of representations can
   be JUDGED objective, and the only candidate rule for fixing an
   irreversible position in a single time is a causal one.
   ADJUDICATION FOR THE ANSWER: Kant defeats Hume at the level of
   CONCEPTUAL ENTITLEMENT - we are entitled to the concept of cause; it
   is not a bastard of the imagination - but he does NOT deliver what
   Hume asked for, namely knowledge of PARTICULAR causal connections.
   That is exactly what Kant intended, since particular laws remain
   empirical.
 ======================================================================
```

⚠️ **Caption.** The adjudication line is the single most transferable sentence in this topic. It converts "Kant answered Hume" from a slogan into a measured verdict.

#### 1. The First Analogy — permanence of substance

**Numbered argument:**
1. All appearances are in time.
2. Time itself cannot be perceived.
3. Time has two modes to be determined — **succession** and **simultaneity** — and both presuppose a *single* time in which they are located.
4. To represent things as being in one and the same time, there must be, *in the appearances themselves*, something that stands proxy for time's own permanence, since time does not change and is not itself an object.
5. That something is **substance**: the persisting real (*das Beharrliche*) of which all change is mere alteration of determinations.
6. ∴ Coming-to-be and passing-away are not the arising and perishing of *substance* but the **alteration** (*Veränderung*) of the *states* of a persisting substance. What changes are accidents; substance does not change, it *has* changing states.
7. ∴ "In all change of appearances substance persists, and its quantum in nature is neither increased nor diminished" (B224).

✅ **Why this defeats Hume without contradicting him.** Hume was right that there is no *impression* of substance, and Kant agrees entirely. Kant's move is transcendental: substance is not something perceived *in* experience but a **condition of perceiving anything as located in one time**. The empiricist demand — "show me the impression" — is therefore the wrong demand, because what is at stake is not a content but a condition.

⚠️ **Presuppositions:** (P1) time is unitary and unperceivable; (P2) time-determination must proceed through a perceptible proxy; (P3) "alteration" is intelligible only against a persisting bearer.

❓ **Strongest objection.** The argument may prove only that *something or other* must be treated as relatively persisting for the purpose of time-determination — not that there is a sempiternal, quantitatively conserved substance. Kant's conservation clause, "its quantum in nature is neither increased nor diminished", looks like Newtonian physics being smuggled into transcendental philosophy.

#### 2. The Second Analogy — causality, the real answer to Hume

**Numbered argument:**
1. Apprehension of the manifold is *always* successive — in both the house case and the ship case my perceptions come one after another. So mere succession-of-perceptions cannot by itself constitute the perception of an objective succession.
2. Yet I do distinguish the house case (subjective order) from the ship case (objective event).
3. The distinction cannot be read off time itself, because time cannot be perceived — the master premise.
4. The distinction must therefore lie in the fact that in the ship case the order of perceptions is **necessary**: B *had to* follow A and could not have preceded it.
5. Necessity of order cannot come from perception — Hume is right about that — so it can come only from the perceptions being subsumed under a **rule** that determines the earlier from the later.
6. Such a rule *is* the concept of cause: "everything that happens presupposes something upon which it follows **according to a rule**" (A189).
7. ∴ The causal principle is a **condition of the possibility of experiencing objective events** — synthetic, a priori, and valid necessarily of all appearances, but **only** of appearances.

⚠️ **Presuppositions:** (P1) the objective/subjective distinction in temporal order is genuine and must be grounded; (P2) irreversibility of apprehension requires a rule; (P3) the only available rule for fixing temporal position is causal.

✅ **The comparison table that answers "how does Kant respond to Hume".**

| Hume's position | Kant's response |
|---|---|
| Causation is constant conjunction plus custom | Causation is an a priori **category**, necessarily applied in order to organise appearances |
| No impression of necessary connexion, so causation is subjective and psychological | "Every event has a cause" is **synthetic a priori** — necessarily true of all possible experience because it is a condition of objective time-determination |
| Induction cannot be justified | The causal principle is not *inductive* but **constitutive**: it is presupposed by any experience of an objective event, so it cannot be a generalisation from such experiences |
| Science rests on mere habit | Science is objectively valid, since its fundamental principles are the mind's own contribution, legislating to nature *formally* |

- ✅ **But** — and this restriction must be written — causality applies **only to phenomena**. Applying it beyond experience, for instance to infer God as a first cause, is illegitimate. The same argument that defeats Hume's scepticism also defeats rationalist dogmatism, and saying so in one sentence is the strongest possible close.

#### 3. The Third Analogy — community or reciprocity

✅ Simultaneity, like succession, cannot be read off unperceivable time. If A and B are simultaneous, I can perceive them in either order — the moon then the earth, or the earth then the moon — but I can also perceive a *non*-simultaneous pair in either order unless something fixes them in one time. What fixes them is **mutual causal influence** (*commercium*): substances are simultaneous only insofar as they stand in thoroughgoing reciprocal determination. This is the schema of the category of **Community**.

⚠️ The Third Analogy is the least examined and the most useful for showing completeness: it demonstrates that the three Analogies exhaust the modes of time rather than being three examples chosen at random.

#### 4. What kind of victory this is — the adjudication

- ✅ **Won:** the *entitlement* to the concept of cause. Causality is not, as Hume held, a bastard of the imagination; it is a condition of objective experience.
- ❌ **Not won:** knowledge of any *particular* causal connection. That bread nourishes, that this collision produces that motion — these remain empirical discoveries, and Kant never claimed otherwise.
- ⚠️ **Why this is not an evasion:** Kant's project was to secure the *form* of nature a priori and to leave the *content* to science. The restriction is therefore a deliberate feature, not a concession extracted under pressure.
- ✅ **The permanent result:** objectivity is a matter of **rule-governed time-determination**. This is why Strawson could rebuild the Analogies without the doctrine of appearances — the Analogies are the part of the *Critique* most nearly independent of transcendental idealism.

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification

> **Claim.** Kant's answer to Hume is delivered not by the bare assertion that causality is a category but by the Second Analogy's argument that objective succession is impossible without a causal rule.
> **Named evidence.** Kant's master premise is that time itself cannot be perceived (A176/B219); his example contrasts the reversible order in which I may survey a house with the irreversible order in which I must perceive a ship drifting downstream (A190–3/B235–8); and he concludes that "everything that happens presupposes something upon which it follows according to a rule" (A189).
> **Analysis.** Since apprehension is successive in both cases, the difference between a subjective sequence and an objective event cannot lie in the succession of my perceptions, and since it cannot be read off unperceivable time either, it must lie in the necessity of the order — a necessity available only through a rule, which is precisely the concept of cause that Hume had declared unearned.
> **Qualification.** Strawson calls the transition from the determined order of apprehension to causal necessitation in nature a "non-sequitur of numbing grossness", and Guyer objects that at most some rule is required rather than the universal causal law; the defensible verdict is therefore that Kant secures the *entitlement* to the concept of cause while leaving particular causal laws empirical, which is exactly the result his own division of form from content required.

#### Exam link

- **2024 Q1(b), 10 marks** is answered from §2, with the entitlement adjudication in §4 as the closing verdict.
- **2025 Q2(c)** — routed to Topic 03 but naming Kant's response — takes Hume's argument first and then §2.
- **2018 Q2(b)** uses §2 and §4 to explain in what sense the understanding legislates to nature and in what sense it does not.

#### UPSC traps

| Trap | Correction |
|---|---|
| "Kant found the necessity in the objects that Hume could not find" | He relocates necessity to the conditions of objective time-determination, not to perceived objects |
| Listing three Analogies without the master premise | The premise explains why there are three and why they are needed |
| Claiming Kant proves particular causal laws | He proves entitlement to the concept; laws remain empirical |
| Using the ship example without the house | The contrast, not the ship alone, carries the argument |
| Omitting the restriction to appearances | The same argument that defeats Hume forbids inferring a first cause |
| Ignoring Strawson | "Critically examine" and "Discuss" both reward a named objection |

#### Revision notes

- General principle: experience is possible only through the representation of a necessary connection of perceptions (B218).
- Master premise: time itself cannot be perceived (A176/B219, A183/B226).
- Three modes of time → three proxies → three Analogies.
- First: substance persists; change is alteration of states, not perishing of substance; quantum conserved (B224).
- Second: reversible house versus irreversible ship; necessity of order requires a rule; that rule is cause (A189).
- Third: simultaneity requires thoroughgoing reciprocity (*commercium*), B256.
- Victory = entitlement to the concept of cause; particular laws remain empirical.
- Objection: Strawson's "non-sequitur of numbing grossness"; Guyer's weaker conclusion; Allison's reply.
- Restriction: valid of appearances only — hence no first cause of the world.

#### CLOSING RECALL FLOW — THE ANALOGIES OF EXPERIENCE

```text
GENERAL PRINCIPLE (B218): experience requires a NECESSARY CONNECTION of
   perceptions
        |
        v
MASTER PREMISE: TIME ITSELF CANNOT BE PERCEIVED (A176/B219)
        |
        v
THREE MODES OF TIME -> THREE PROXIES IN APPEARANCES
   DURATION ...... SUBSTANCE persists; change = ALTERATION of states;
                   quantum conserved (B224)     [answers Hume on substance]
   SUCCESSION .... CAUSAL RULE: house REVERSIBLE vs ship IRREVERSIBLE;
                   "everything that happens presupposes something upon
                   which it follows ACCORDING TO A RULE" (A189)
   SIMULTANEITY .. COMMUNITY / commercium: thoroughgoing reciprocity (B256)
        |
        v
ANSWER TO HUME: constant conjunction + custom -> A PRIORI CATEGORY;
   causal principle is CONSTITUTIVE, not inductive
        |
        +--> OBJECTION: STRAWSON "non-sequitur of numbing grossness";
        |    GUYER: only SOME rule, not the universal law
        +--> REPLY: Allison - conditions for JUDGING a succession objective
        |
        v
ADJUDICATION: Kant wins the ENTITLEMENT to the concept of cause; he does
   NOT deliver particular causal laws - and never claimed to
RESTRICTION: valid of APPEARANCES ONLY -> no inference to a first cause
ANSWER LINE: objectivity is rule-governed TIME-DETERMINATION
```

### SESSION 9 — THE REFUTATION OF IDEALISM: OUTER EXPERIENCE AS THE CONDITION OF INNER EXPERIENCE

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition:** Descartes thought he could be certain of his own mind while doubting the outside world. Kant argues that the certainty Descartes claims about his own mental history already requires something permanent outside him — so the doubt destroys its own starting point.

**Technical definition:** the **Refutation of Idealism** (B274–9, added in the second edition of 1787) is Kant's proof that "the mere, but empirically determined, consciousness of my own existence proves the existence of objects in space outside me". It works by applying the First Analogy to inner sense: determination of position in time requires something persistent in perception; nothing merely inner can supply it; therefore something outer must.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> The Refutation is the moment transcendental idealism reveals itself as an **empirical realism**: Kant does not answer the sceptic by producing better evidence for the external world, he shows that the sceptic's own premise — determinate knowledge of his inner life — already presupposes what he proposed to doubt.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

- **problematic idealism (Descartes) versus dogmatic idealism (Berkeley)** — Kant's own taxonomy at B274
- **"scandal of philosophy" (Bxxxix n.)** — with the footnote provenance attached
- **empirically determined consciousness of my own existence** — the full premise, not "I think"
- **something persistent in perception** — the First Analogy doing double duty
- **"Inner experience in general is possible only through outer experience in general" (B278–9)** — the corollary
- **empirically external, not noumenally external** — the exact size of the conclusion

**How to use them:** name Kant's two enemies before the proof. Candidates who write "Kant refutes idealism" without saying *which* idealism cannot then explain why the Aesthetic answers Berkeley and the Refutation answers Descartes — and that division of labour is half the marks.

#### Plain-language visual — the inversion

```text
   DESCARTES' ORDER OF CERTAINTY
   [1] I am certain of MY OWN MIND (the cogito)
   [2] ... and only afterwards, and doubtfully, of BODIES OUTSIDE ME
                              |
   KANT'S REPLY: look again at [1]. What exactly are you certain of?
   Not a timeless flash, but a DATED inner life - "this thought came
   AFTER that one". You are claiming DETERMINATE TIME-ORDER.
                              |
   But (First Analogy) determinate time-order requires something
   PERSISTENT against which the flux is measured.
                              |
   The flux itself cannot supply the fixed point - the persisting thing
   cannot be one of the very items whose position is to be determined.
                              |
                              v
   THEREFORE the persistent must be OUTSIDE me, in space.
   [1] ALREADY CONTAINS [2]. The order of certainty is INVERTED.
```

**In simple words:** you cannot time a race using only the runners. You need a clock that is not itself one of the runners. Descartes tried to time his mental life using only mental events, and Kant's point is that this is not a difficulty to be overcome by ingenuity but an impossibility in principle. Something outside the flux has to stand still.

| Remember first | Meaning |
|---|---|
| B-edition only | added 1787; never attribute it to the 1781 text |
| Two enemies, two weapons | Berkeley answered by the Aesthetic; Descartes by the Refutation |
| The premise is Descartes' own | determinate inner time-consciousness |
| The engine is the First Analogy | permanence is required for time-determination |
| The conclusion is empirical | Kant proves the desk, not the noumenal desk |

> **One-line memory:** self-knowledge is parasitic on world-knowledge, not the reverse.

#### 🖼️ VISUAL — the taxonomy, the numbered proof, and the three objections

  1787: Kant answers twice - he rewrites the Fourth Paralogism as a
  direct PROOF of the external world, and he complains in the second-
  edition Preface that "it remains A SCANDAL OF PHILOSOPHY ... that the
  existence of things outside us ... must be accepted merely on FAITH"
  (Bxxxix n.).   [NOTE: this is a FOOTNOTE IN THE PREFACE, not in the
  Refutation itself.]

                                    "I am" is indubitable
