- **Transcendental** = concerning a priori conditions (legitimate). **Transcendent** = beyond all possible experience (illegitimate). **Transcendental illusion** = the natural, unavoidable semblance produced when a regulative demand is read constitutively; criticism prevents deception, not appearance.
- **Two-sided result:** science vindicated; rational psychology, cosmology and theology closed as sciences; "I had to deny knowledge in order to make room for faith" (Bxxx).
- **Chronology:** 1770 Dissertation (not yet critical) → silent decade → 1781 A → 1781 Garve–Feder review → 1783 *Prolegomena* → 1787 B → 1788 *Practical Reason* → 1790 *Judgment* (outside this item).
- **Edition rule:** Refutation of Idealism B274–9 and the B-Deduction B129–69 are **B-only**; the original Fourth Paralogism is **A-only**.

### B. The two distinctions and the synthetic a priori

- **Analytic** = predicate covertly contained, explicative, denial self-contradictory. **Synthetic** = predicate added, ampliative, denial not contradictory.
- **A priori** = necessity + **strict universality** (B3–4). **A posteriori** = contingent, only comparative universality. B1: cognition **begins** with experience but does not all **arise** from it.
- **The empty cell:** analytic a posteriori is impossible, since containment makes empirical warrant idle.
- **Showcases, each argued.** *7 + 5 = 12*: the concept of the sum contains only their union, not twelve — brought in by counting in pure intuition. *The straight line is the shortest*: a quality cannot yield a quantity. *Every event has a cause*: the concept of an event contains no determining antecedent, and the principle cannot be induced without circularity.
- **Metaphysics aspires to the class and fails** — which is why the grading of the examples is itself the argument.
- **Hume's fork superimposed:** relations of ideas = analytic a priori; matters of fact = synthetic a posteriori; **no third tine** — hence custom.
- **The question is *quid juris***, not *quid facti*: the existence of the class is the datum.
- ⚠️ **Qualification:** non-Euclidean geometry and relativity damaged the geometrical showcase, not the class.

### C. The change of standpoint and the reconciliation formula

- **Hypothesis (Bxvi–xviii):** objects, **as objects of possible experience**, conform to our a priori forms. Things in themselves expressly exempted. ⚠️ "Copernican revolution" is the commentators' label.
- **The analogy correctly stated:** Copernicus explained apparent celestial motion by moving the **observer**; the comparison is about the direction of explanation. He *displaced* the earth, so "man at the centre" inverts it.
- **Supreme principle (A158/B197):** the conditions of the possibility of experience are at the same time conditions of the possibility of the objects of experience.
- **Yield:** mathematics explained; the fundamental principles of natural science explained; metaphysical failure explained **and predicted**.
- **Formula (A51/B75):** thoughts without content are empty (against rationalism); intuitions without concepts are blind (against empiricism). **Two stems:** receptive sensibility supplies content, spontaneous understanding supplies rule. "The understanding can intuit nothing, and the senses can think nothing."
- **Not a 50:50 compromise:** empiricist about the source of content, rationalist about the source of necessity.
- **Four denials:** no creation of empirical objects; no noumenal conformity; space is empirically real; not "raw data plus imposed scheme".
- **2023 protocol:** quote the printed "Precepts" line → restore A51/B75 → note the substitution and transposition → answer.

### D. Transcendental Aesthetic — space and time

- **Space** = form of **outer** sense. **Time** = form of **inner** sense and therefore of **all** representations. Both are **pure intuitions**.
- **Rejected:** Newton's self-subsistent containers; Leibniz's confused relations among things in themselves; empirical abstraction.
- **Metaphysical exposition:** (1) not derived from experience, (2) necessary → **apriority**; (3) one space, parts by limitation, (4) given as an infinite magnitude → **intuitivity**. Parallel arguments for time.
- **Transcendental exposition:** space grounds **geometry**; time grounds **arithmetic** and the pure doctrine of motion.
- **2019 wording:** "transcendence" = the a priori and transcendentally ideal status, **not** existence beyond experience.
- **Double result:** **transcendental ideality** (nothing apart from our sensibility — this is what kills Newton and Leibniz together) **with empirical reality** (objectively valid of every appearance — this is what kills the Berkeley charge). Never quote one alone.
- **Load-bearing asymmetry:** only time is present in every representation; space is not. This is why the Schematism uses time.
- ⚠️ Non-Euclidean geometry weakens the argument **from geometry**; the metaphysical exposition survives.

### E. The metaphysical deduction and the twelve categories

- **Premise:** the understanding is a faculty of judging, so the functions of unity in judgement exhaust it.
- **Category** = the concept of the synthesis of a manifold given in intuition, in accordance with a logical function of judgement. Worked case: the **hypothetical** form becomes **causality and dependence**.
- **Quantity:** unity, plurality, totality → extensive magnitude. **Quality:** reality, negation, limitation → intensive magnitude, degree. **Relation:** inherence and subsistence, causality and dependence, community → objective temporal relations. **Modality:** possibility, existence, necessity → the relation of the object to **our faculty of cognition**, yielding the Postulates of Empirical Thought.
- ⚠️ **Modality trap:** no modal category licenses an inference to a necessary *being* — the confusion the Fourth Antinomy and the cosmological proof exploit.
- **Third of each group** = the first two combined; a special act of the understanding is still required.
- **Unschematised categories** are thinkable but determine no object.
- **Objection:** why exactly twelve? **Reply:** claimed completeness of the table of judgements. **Residual:** detach the Deduction's **strategy** from its **inventory**.

### F. The transcendental deduction and apperception

- ***Quid facti*** (which concepts) versus ***quid juris*** (by what right). Empirical concepts pose no problem; pure ones do.
- **Premise (B131–2):** "the *I think* **must be able to** accompany all my representations." Modal, not continuous.
- **Steps:** original and transcendental unity → combination is never given by the senses, always an act of spontaneity → one consciousness requires rule-governed synthesis → the rules are the categories → an object is "that in the concept of which the manifold of a given intuition is united" → objective validity for all objects of possible experience.
- **Apperception is:** formal, original, transcendental, a condition, a **function**. **Is not:** an observation, an impression, Hume's bundle, a substance, knowledge of myself as I am in myself.
- **Two flanking errors:** Hume sought the self among impressions (it is a condition, not a content); rational psychology inferred a soul-substance (the Paralogism).
- **Architecture for 2022:** Aesthetic gives the forms of intuition → Deduction justifies the categories through apperceptive unity → Schematism mediates temporal application. ⚠️ The transcendental exposition does **not** deduce apperception.
- **"Understanding makes Nature"** = nature **formally** considered, the law-governed unity of appearances. ❌ Not the matter of sensation, not particular empirical laws, not private imagination. **Objection:** this collapses nature into mind. **Reply:** transcendental idealism *is* empirical realism.
- **2018 ruling:** Hegel accepts, radicalises and rejects — a culmination in extending constitutive idealism, an attempted **overcoming** in rejecting Kant's limits.

### G. The Schematism

- **Problem:** subsumption requires homogeneity; categories and appearances are **heterogeneous**. The Deduction proved *that* categories apply, not *how*.
- **Solution:** a **third thing** (*ein Drittes*) — the **transcendental schema**, a **transcendental determination of time**, produced by the productive imagination. Homogeneous with each in a *different respect*, not partly each.
- **Why time:** it is a priori *and* the form of inner sense, hence in every representation. **Space fails** the universality test.
- **Schema ≠ image:** triangle (rule of construction versus a determinate drawn figure); five ("a method of representing a multitude in one image"); dog (the rule for a four-footed animal in general). **Rule versus product.**
- **Four aspects — SCOR-S:** time-**series** → number; time-**content** → degree; time-**order** → permanence, succession by rule, coexistence by rule; time-**sum-total** → at some time, at a determinate time, at all times.
- **Presuppositions:** P1 time is in every representation; P2 subsumption requires homogeneity (contestable); P3 the productive imagination is a genuine third faculty; **P4 no schema, no sense for objects** — which is why the unschematised category is thinkable and not knowable.
- **Objections:** heterogeneity reappears inside the schema; **Hegel** on artificial division and rejoining (the deepest); redundancy (Warren, Paton) — best answered **functionally**, since the Analogies *are* the Relation schemata at work.
- **Kant's concession:** "an art concealed in the depths of the human soul" (A141/B180–1).

### H. The Analogies of Experience

- **General principle (B218):** experience is possible only through the representation of a necessary connection of perceptions. **Regulative** for intuition, **constitutive** for experience.
- **Master premise (A176/B219, A183/B226):** **time itself cannot be perceived**. Three modes of time → three proxies → exactly three Analogies.
- **First — duration.** Time-determination needs a proxy for time's permanence: **substance**. Change is **alteration** (*Veränderung*) of states, not coming-to-be of substance; "its quantum in nature is neither increased nor diminished" (B224). Defeats Hume without contradicting him: substance is a condition, not a content. ❓ Objection: it may prove only *relative* persistence, and the conservation clause looks Newtonian.
- **Second — succession.** House **reversible** (subjective order of a coexisting object) versus ship **irreversible** (an objective event). Apprehension is successive in both, so the difference lies in the **necessity of the order**, which requires a rule: "everything that happens presupposes something upon which it follows according to a rule" (A189).
- **Third — simultaneity.** Substances are simultaneous only in **thoroughgoing reciprocity** (*commercium*), B256.
- **Hume → Kant:** custom → a priori category; psychological necessity → synthetic a priori principle; unjustifiable induction → constitutive condition; habit → objectively valid science.
- **Adjudication:** Kant wins the **entitlement** to the concept of cause and does **not** deliver particular causal laws. ❓ Strawson's "non-sequitur of numbing grossness"; Guyer's weaker conclusion; Allison's reply.
- **Restriction:** appearances only — so the same argument forbids inferring a first cause.

### I. The Refutation of Idealism

- **B274–9, second edition 1787**, provoked by the 1781 Garve–Feder review; "scandal of philosophy" is a **footnote in the Preface** (Bxxxix n.).
- **Taxonomy (B274):** *problematic* idealism (Descartes — existence of outer objects doubtful and indemonstrable) versus *dogmatic* idealism (Berkeley — matter impossible, space illusory). **Berkeley answered by the Aesthetic; Descartes by the Refutation.** Kant calls problematic idealism rational and philosophically respectable.
- **Thesis (B275):** the mere but empirically determined consciousness of my own existence proves the existence of objects in space outside me.
- **Proof:** determinate inner time-order → requires something persistent (First Analogy) → the persistent cannot be inner, since the flux cannot measure itself → therefore outside me in space → outer experience is a **condition** of inner experience, not an inference from it. **Corollary (B278–9):** inner experience in general is possible only through outer experience in general.
- **P3 does the work** and is the step examiners probe.
- **Objections:** Strawson and Guyer — at most I must *represent* a persistent; the Preface footnote revision; only **empirically** external objects. Kant **accepts** the third: empirical realism with transcendental idealism.
- **Comparison:** Moore *blocks* the sceptic's question ("here is one hand…"); Kant *turns* it.

### J. Phenomena, noumena and the limits of knowledge

- **Appearance (*Erscheinung*) ≠ illusion (*Schein*).** Illusion is distinguished **within** appearances by empirical criteria.
- **Phenomena:** objects under space, time and the categories; knowable; the objects of science. **Thing in itself:** the object considered apart from our sensible conditions.
- **Negative noumenon:** a **limiting concept** (*Grenzbegriff*) — legitimate. **Positive noumenon:** an object of intellectual intuition, which we lack — problematic, not cognised. ⚠️ Reading the negative as the positive makes every objection from Jacobi to Hegel irresistible.
- **Thinkable but not knowable** — thinking needs only the logical function (Schematism P4); knowing needs a schema.
- **Two-world versus two-aspect:** state the shared limit first, then choose with reasons.
- **Affection problem / Jacobi's dilemma:** a genuine tension. ❌ Never assert noumenal causation as Kant's own doctrine — the commonest Kant error in scripts.
- **Hegel's challenge and the adjudication:** his objection succeeds against a **knowledge-claim** reading of the noumenon and fails against the **limiting-concept** reading.
- **Three consequences:** science secured; speculative metaphysics closed; freedom becomes **thinkable**, not cognised.

### K. Transcendental illusion, the Ideas, the Paralogisms and the Ideal

- **Logical illusion vanishes on exposure; transcendental illusion does not** — it is grounded in reason itself (the moon on the horizon).
- **Reason's principle:** if the conditioned is given, the whole series of conditions, and hence the unconditioned, is given. The ambiguity in "is given" generates the whole Dialectic.
- **Three Ideas from three relations:** soul (categorical), world (hypothetical), God (disjunctive) — not an arbitrary list.
- **Regulative** (maxims of systematic enquiry — legitimate, indispensable) versus **constitutive** (claims about existing objects — illegitimate). "Perfectly good instructions and perfectly bad objects."
- **Four Paralogisms:** substantiality, simplicity, personality, ideality. **Fallacy = equivocation** on the middle term, between the "I" as **logical subject of thought** and the "I" as a **real object**. Worked case: "that which cannot be thought otherwise than as subject… is therefore substance" moves from the form of thought to a claim about existence. The vehicle of all concepts cannot be one of the items carried.
- **What survives:** the unity of apperception is real and indispensable; only the inference to a knowable soul fails. I know myself only as I **appear** to myself.
- **Ideal of Pure Reason:** complete determination → the sum-total of all possible predicates → the **most real being** (*ens realissimum*) → hypostatised. This is why all three God-proofs converge, and therefore why one refutation kills all three.

### L. The Antinomies

- **Definition:** thesis and antithesis each apparently demonstrable, arising when the cosmological Idea is treated as an object in itself.
- **When reason enters (2020):** when the legitimate search for conditions demands an absolutely unconditioned, **completed** world-series and treats that totality as an object given in itself.
- **Construction (2022):** one cosmological Idea per category-group; both sides proved by ***reductio***; both share the premise that the world is a **given whole**; the conflict is exhibited as **necessary**. "Thesis"/"Antithesis" are **Kant's own printed headings**.
- **The four:** beginning and limits vs infinity; simple parts vs nothing simple; freedom alongside nature vs nature alone; a necessary being vs no necessary being.
- **Mathematical (1–2):** homogeneous series → **both false**; the world-whole is no object; the regress is **indefinite**. **Dynamical (3–4):** heterogeneous series → **both may be true** at different standpoints.
- **Third Antinomy:** the antithesis holds of appearances; the thesis is thinkable of the same agent as noumenon. ⚠️ **Room, not proof.**
- **Fourth Antinomy:** no necessary being **within** the series; an intelligible ground is neither contradicted nor proved.
- **Significance:** supersensible metaphysics is not a science; the critical turn is motivated; the antinomies are the most dramatic argument against rationalist cosmology; and they are the **indirect proof** of transcendental idealism. ❓ **Limit:** the proof needs there to be no third diagnosis, and Hegel supplies one — contradiction as productive.
- **2020 ruling:** the antinomies culminate **transcendental realism about appearances**; the phenomena/noumena distinction is the **cure**, not the cause.

### M. The critique of the proofs for God's existence

- **Framework:** God lies beyond possible experience; the categories have objective employment only through schemata; therefore all theoretical proofs fail. **Only three** speculative routes exist — the exhaustiveness claim is itself an argument.
- **Ontological:** "being is obviously not a real predicate" (A598/B626), where **real = content-adding** (*Bestimmung*, from *res*). Existence **posits**, it does not **determine**. "A hundred real thalers do not contain the least coin more than a hundred possible thalers" — the concept is unchanged. **Corollary:** rejecting subject and predicate together yields no contradiction.
- **Cosmological:** **Blow 1** — causality misapplied beyond experience, since the totality of existents is not an event in time. **Blow 2, decisive** — identifying the necessary being with the most real being **is** the ontological argument. A "nest of dialectical assumptions".
- **Physico-theological:** ✅ Kant's respect — the oldest, clearest and most in conformity with common reason. **Ceiling:** an **architect** ordering pre-existing material, not a creator; a finite effect licenses only a proportionate cause. **Borrowing:** needs an infinite or necessary cause → cosmological → ontological.
- **Dependency chain:** design → cosmological → ontological → fails → **all three fall together**.
- **Positive residue, bounded:** theoretical **agnosticism**, never atheism; practical reason **postulates** God (with freedom and immortality) as a condition of the highest good; Bxxx. ⚠️ Stop there — Kantian ethics is outside the printed clause.
- **Assessment (for "critically examine"):** devastating against Descartes and Anselm; ❓ contested against modal versions (Malcolm, Plantinga); vindicated by the later formalisation of existence in the existential quantifier.

### N. Exam craft — comparison, criticism, directives, provenance, traps

- **Comparative axis:** *where does necessity come from?* — reason (rationalism), custom (Hume), the conditions of objecthood-for-us (Kant). Seven rows: source of knowledge, causality, synthetic a priori, metaphysics, God, things in themselves, self.
- **Six criticisms with replies and residuals:** Hegel on the empty in-itself; why exactly twelve categories; the Berkeley charge (decisively answered by B274); Jacobi's affection problem (a genuine tension); two-world dualism; scheme and content. **A criticism without a reply, or a reply without a residual, forfeits the assessment mark.**
- **Directives:** "How is X possible?" → the transcendental form *given F, and F only if C, therefore C*. "Critically examine" → assessment dominates. "Discuss" → 60:40 with one named objection. "How far do you agree?" → a ruling **with a distinction**. "Give reasons" → at least two independent grounds.
- **Provenance:** cite by **A/B pagination**; *Glaube* = faith/belief; "real predicate" = content-adding; "Copernican revolution" is the commentators' label; *unterbrach* = interrupted, and in the *Prolegomena*; "Thesis"/"Antithesis" are Kant's own headings.
- **Ten-line trap sheet:** transcendental ≠ transcendent · a priori ≠ analytic · the Copernican analogy is about the direction of explanation · ideality **with** reality · metaphysical deduction identifies, transcendental deduction justifies · apperception is a function, keep "must be able to" · "makes Nature" = form, not matter · schema = rule, image = product · mathematical both false, dynamical both may be true, freedom is room not proof · Kant is theoretically agnostic, and ethics is outside this clause.
- **Answer spine:** transcendental question → numbered doctrine with A/B references → what it explains that neither predecessor could → the restriction to appearances → one named objection with reply and residual → an **earned** graded verdict.
- **Memory chain:** synthetic a priori → Copernican standpoint → space and time as forms → twelve categories → deduction via apperception → schematism as time-determination → analogies → refutation of idealism → phenomena/noumena → ideas of reason → paralogisms → antinomies → God-proofs → postulates.
- **Qualified conclusion.** ⚠️ Kant's critical philosophy is one argument with two faces: the demonstration that secures mathematics and the fundamental principles of natural science as synthetic a priori cognition is the same demonstration that forbids theoretical knowledge of soul, world-totality and God, because the forms that make necessity possible reach only what can appear to us. Its permanent achievements are the relocation of necessity to the conditions of objecthood-for-us and the demonstration that objectivity is rule-governed time-determination; its permanent costs are that the completeness of the table of categories is inherited rather than proved, that the affection of sensibility by things in themselves cannot be described in the terms the system allows, and that the boundary between appearance and reality can be stated more securely than it can be explained.

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 7/74: Transcendental versus transcendent, and the shape of every argument
==============================================================================
TRANSCENDENTAL  = concerning the a priori CONDITIONS under which objects
                  can be given to us          ........... LEGITIMATE

                  unity of apperception; transcendental exposition
TRANSCENDENT    = purporting to apply concepts BEYOND all possible

                  e.g. inferring a soul-substance from the "I think";
                  proving a first cause of the world
TRANSCENDENTAL  = the NATURAL and UNAVOIDABLE semblance that arises when

                  knowledge ..... DIAGNOSABLE, NOT CURABLE
                  compare: the moon still looks larger on the horizon
                  after we know why
        |

THE STANDING ARGUMENT FORM - EVERY KANTIAN PROOF IN THIS TOPIC
   1 identify a fact of cognition both parties accept
       there IS mathematics | there IS a distinction between an
       objective event and a merely subjective sequence | my
       representations are all MINE
   2 ask what conditions must hold if that fact is to be possible
   3 show the conditions cannot be supplied by the OBJECTS, since then
     they would be contingent and a posteriori
   4 conclude that they are supplied by the SUBJECT as A PRIORI FORMS
   5 NOTE THE PRICE: the conclusion holds for objects AS THEY CAN
     APPEAR TO US, and for nothing beyond
        |
        v
STEP 5 IS WHERE MARKS ARE WON AND LOST. A transcendental argument buys
   NECESSITY at the cost of SCOPE. An answer that reports the necessity
   and omits the restriction has reported half of Kant.
TRAP: using "transcendental" and "transcendent" as synonyms destroys the
   whole Dialectic, because Kant's diagnosis is precisely the slide from
   the first to the second.

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 9/74: The synthetic a priori showcases, argued not listed
==============================================================================
ARITHMETIC   "7 + 5 = 12"
   SYNTHETIC because the concept of the SUM of 7 and 5 contains only the
     thought of UNITING them; the number twelve is not thought in it and
     must be brought in by counting in PURE INTUITION
   A PRIORI because no experiment could falsify it and no counting
     establishes its NECESSITY; it holds strictly universally
GEOMETRY     "the straight line between two points is the shortest"
   SYNTHETIC because "straight" is a QUALITY and "shortest" a QUANTITY;
     no analysis of the quality yields the quantity
   A PRIORI because it is CONSTRUCTED in pure spatial intuition and
     holds of every object of outer sense
FUNDAMENTAL SCIENCE  "every event has a cause"; "in all change of
   appearances substance persists"
   SYNTHETIC because the concept of an EVENT (something that begins to
     be) does not contain the concept of a determining antecedent
   A PRIORI because it cannot be induced from experience without
     circularity - objective time-order already presupposes it
METAPHYSICS AS ATTEMPTED  "the world has a beginning in time"; "the soul
   is a simple substance"
   AMPLIATIVE, certainly - but HERE THE CLASS FAILS. Metaphysics
   ASPIRES to synthetic a priori status and cannot earn it.
        |

THE GRADING IS THE ARGUMENT: mathematics is the SECURE case; the
   principles of natural science are the case that ANSWERS HUME;
   speculative metaphysics is the case that COLLAPSES.
QUALIFICATION, STATED HONESTLY: non-Euclidean geometries and their
   physical application removed the assumption that ONE Euclidean
   geometry is uniquely valid of experience. "7 + 5 = 12" is untouched;
   "the straight line is the shortest" AS A TRUTH ABOUT PHYSICAL SPACE

==============================================================================
HUME'S FORK
   RELATIONS OF IDEAS ...... discoverable by thought alone; denial is
                             self-contradictory; = ANALYTIC A PRIORI
   MATTERS OF FACT ......... contrary always conceivable; established
                             only through cause and effect;

CONSEQUENCE FOR HUME: causal necessity cannot be a relation of ideas
   (its denial is conceivable) and cannot be given in a matter of fact
   (no impression of necessary connexion) -> therefore it is a FELT
   DETERMINATION of the mind, i.e. CUSTOM; and metaphysics, containing
   neither abstract reasoning about quantity nor experimental reasoning
   about fact, must be committed to the flames.
        |
        v
KANT'S RESPONSE IN ONE MOVE: the third box is OCCUPIED, so the FORK IS
   INCOMPLETE. He does not widen the fork to save metaphysics; he shows
   the fork was never exhaustive - and the very class that saves
   mathematics and physics is the class metaphysics CANNOT reach.
        |
        v
THE QUESTION IS quid juris, NOT quid facti
   quid facti  the EXISTENCE of synthetic a priori judgements is the
               DATUM; Kant exhibits instances
   quid juris  BY WHAT RIGHT do we assert a judgement that goes beyond
               its concept and yet claims necessity?
   ANSWER: because the predicate is not extracted from the object but is
   a CONDITION under which alone the object could be given to us.
TRAP: claiming Kant PROVED the existence of the class from first
   principles. He exhibits instances and then asks how they are
   possible.

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 11/74: The change of standpoint, the supreme principle and four denials
==============================================================================
THE HYPOTHESIS (Bxvi-xviii)
   Hitherto it was assumed that all our cognition must conform to
   objects; on that assumption every attempt to establish something
   about objects A PRIORI failed.
   "Let us therefore try whether we may not have better success ... if
   we suppose that OBJECTS MUST CONFORM TO OUR COGNITION."
   RESTRICTING CLAUSE, part of the hypothesis and not a later
   concession: what conforms are objects AS OBJECTS OF POSSIBLE
   EXPERIENCE - appearances. THINGS IN THEMSELVES ARE EXEMPTED.
   PROVENANCE: Kant invokes Copernicus at Bxvi; the LABEL "Copernican
   revolution" is the COMMENTATORS'.
        |
   THE ANALOGY, STATED PRECISELY: Copernicus stopped explaining the
   apparent motion of the heavens by moving the HEAVENS and began
   explaining it by moving the OBSERVER. Kant likewise explains a priori
   knowledge by the CONSTITUTION OF THE KNOWER.
   NOT the analogy: that man is the centre of the universe - Copernicus
   DISPLACED the earth, so the popular reading INVERTS the comparison.
        |
        v
SUPREME PRINCIPLE OF ALL SYNTHETIC JUDGEMENTS (A158/B197)
   THE CONDITIONS OF THE POSSIBILITY OF EXPERIENCE IN GENERAL ARE AT
   THE SAME TIME CONDITIONS OF THE POSSIBILITY OF THE OBJECTS OF
   EXPERIENCE.
   -> anything that must hold if experience is possible must also hold
      of everything that can be an object within that experience
        |
        v
WHAT THE HYPOTHESIS EXPLAINS - THE TEST KANT SETS IT
   1 MATHEMATICS: space as our form of outer intuition makes geometry
     both ampliative and necessary
   2 FUNDAMENTAL SCIENCE: categories as rules of unification make the
     persistence of substance and the causal rule necessary of all
     appearances
   3 THE FAILURE OF METAPHYSICS: forms valid only of what can appear
     explain not only THAT metaphysics fails but WHY it fails in the
     systematic ways catalogued in the Dialectic
